Case

\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}
. Under this assumption, most of the cases are analogous to
the corresponding ones for user-mode execution. Even in this case, we go by induction on the
rules. We just show some among the most important cases:

-

Case

.
The assumption is

\sigma\vdash_{w}{(\langle x\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{{:}{=}}}}}\mathtt{E}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{;\,}}}\mathtt{D},\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F,w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau)}\to\\
{{(\langle\mathtt{D},{\rho[{x}\leftarrow{\llbracket\mathtt{E}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}}]},\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F,w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau)}},

from the assumption
\chi
, we deduce that is

{{(\langle x\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{{:}{=}}}}}\mathtt{E}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{;\,}}}\eta(\mathtt{D}),\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F^{\prime},w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})}}

so the same rule can be applied on
\chi
to show:

\eta(\sigma)\vdash_{w}{(\langle x\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{{:}{=}}}}}\mathtt{E}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{;\,}}}\mathtt{D},\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F^{\prime},w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})}\to\\
{{(\langle\eta(\mathtt{D}),{\rho[{x}\leftarrow{\llbracket\mathtt{E}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}}]},\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F^{\prime},w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})}}

and the claim comes from the observation that the command of the target configuration is exactly
\eta(\mathtt{D})
.

-

Case

.
The assumption is

\sigma\vdash_{w}{(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{call}}}\ \mathtt{E}(\mathtt{F}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{F}_{k}){\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{;\,}}}\mathtt{D},\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F,w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau)}\to\\
{(\langle w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau(\llbracket\mathtt{E}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}),\rho_{0}^{\prime},\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:{(\langle\mathtt{D},\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F,w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau)})},

where
\rho_{0}^{\prime}
is a shorthand for

\rho_{0}[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\leftarrow\llbracket\mathtt{F}_{1}\rrbracket_{\rho,w},\dots,\llbracket\mathtt{F}_{1}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}]

and from the premise of the rule, we obtain that there is
{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{f}}}\in\mathsf{FunId}_{\mathtt{k}}
such that

\llbracket\mathtt{E}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}=w({\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{f}}})
. Thus, from the definition of
w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau
,
we deduce that
w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau=\tau({\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{f}}})
.
We observe that
\chi
is:

{{(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{fence}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{;\,}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{call}}}\ \mathtt{E}(\mathtt{F}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{F}_{k}){\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{;\,}}}\eta(\mathtt{D}),\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F^{\prime},w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})}}

Thanks to these observations,
we can show that the application of the
rule and of the
rule
to
\chi
show:

\eta(\sigma)\vdash_{w}{(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{call}}}\ \mathtt{E}(\mathtt{F}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{F}_{k}){\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{;\,}}}\mathtt{D},\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F^{\prime},w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})}\to^{2}\\
{{(\langle w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime}(\llbracket\mathtt{E}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}),\rho_{0}^{\prime},\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:{(\langle\eta(\mathtt{D}),\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F^{\prime},w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})})}},

To conclude, we must observe that
w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime}(\llbracket\mathtt{E}\rrbracket_{\rho,w})=\eta(\tau({\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{f}}}))
. This is a consequence of the assumptions
\llbracket\mathtt{E}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}=w({\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{f}}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{f}}}\in\mathsf{FunId}_{\mathtt{k}}
and
\tau\precsim\tau^{\prime}
.

∎

the stack of configurations is not-empty, i.e.
it is like

The proof goes by cases on
\mathtt{C}

of system calls.

w\vdash_{\sigma}(\langle\eta({\text{{*}}\mathtt{E}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{{:}{=}}}}}{\mathtt{F}}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.2383,0.4336}{\mathtt{;\,}}}\mathtt{D}),\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F^{\prime},{{\mu}(}w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime}),b_{\!\mathit{ms}}):S\mathbin{\hbox to35.27pt{\vbox to20.35pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 14.63582pt\lower-9.86601pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}

(\langle\eta(\mathtt{D}),\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:\langle\overline{{{\mu}(}w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})}(p),\rho,\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F^{\prime},\overline{{{\mu}(}w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})},b_{\!\mathit{ms}}\lor b^{\prime}):S

where
p=\llbracket\mathtt{F}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}^{\mathsf{Addr}}
,

v=\llbracket\mathtt{F}\rrbracket_{\rho,w}
.
And we must ensure that the loaded program
(namely
\overline{{{\mu}(}w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})}(p)
)
is equal to
\eta(\mathtt{C}^{\prime})
for some command.
From the premise of the rule
that has
been applied, we deduce that
p\in\underline{w}(\mathsf{FunId}_{\mathtt{k}})

means that there is a function in
\mathsf{FunId}_{\mathtt{k}}

such that
w({\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{f}}})=p
.
From
Remark

7
, we deduce that

\overline{{{\mu}(}w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime})}=w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime\prime}

for some
\tau^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\mathsf{FunId}}\tau^{\prime}\sim_{\mathsf{FunId}}\tau
.
By definition of
\cdot\mathrel{\diamond}\cdot
,
this also means that
\overline{w\mathrel{\diamond}\tau^{\prime\prime}}(p)=\tau(p)

that satisfies the requirement by hypothesis on
\sigma
.

∎

Lemma 17
.

For every pair of configurations

\phi=(\langle\mathtt{C}_{1},\rho_{1},\mathtt{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0547,0.4414,0.3477}{\mathtt{s}}}}\rangle:F_{1},{{\mu}_{1}m_{1}},{b_{\!\mathit{ms}}}_{1}):
